# Validation record

## October 26, 2026: Darwin boot-session process namespace

Pinned XNU 12377.1.9 source shows that calendar changes adjust the boot time exported by `kern.boottime`, while `kern.bootsessionuuid` exposes a read-only UUID generated during boot initialization. Its exported timeval is zero-initialized, so the padding hypothesis is unsupported by that source. The earlier full SSH run's generic “original worker namespace unavailable” diagnostic combined a capture error and a namespace mismatch; its log cannot establish which branch occurred. That failed run remains failed. This change fixes the proven discriminator contract defect without claiming to reproduce that historical event. Recovery now distinguishes the two refusal paths.

Native ARM64 Darwin 25 race validation passes the full usage package: 35 named runs, 33 passes and two fixture-only top-level skips. This includes the existing native layout/timebase and live memory/CPU observations, enabled real gated root/child/grandchild CPU ancestry, ten malformed/unavailable UUID controls, different/obsolete namespace ownership refusal, and three separately initialized execs observing the same exact parent identity. A strengthened native-format assertion also passes its focused independent-process rerun. All 46 selected SSH artifact/status named race cases pass, including real helper exit, exact published-artifact recovery, live-worker veto, hostile-envelope refusal and unchanged artifacts/receipt/pending intent for changed or old-format namespaces. All 32 selected Manager CPU named race cases pass, including the enabled bounded real native credit integration; affected usage/SSH/Manager vet passes. Commands use Go 1.27.3, one Go process worker and package parallelism one. Review evidence is retained under ignored `.cache/darwin-boot-identity/` in the isolated worktree.

Initial sandboxed native attempts failed because the boot-session sysctl was unavailable; identity capture refused rather than falling back. The passing race gates ran with native host access. No clock or security-setting changes were made. The new fixed 37-byte read accepts only an exact uppercase nonnil UUID and never translates retained `darwin:boot:` records. Their cleanup/usage/CPU ownership remains uncertain. These are component/Manager results, not a full SSH package rerun, public CLI/daemon acceptance, Linux/Intel execution, reboot testing or broader model/proof composition. The abstract CPU model and counter derivation are unchanged, but shared `internal/usage` source affects CPU anchor capture, collection and Manager actual-trace/native acceptance dependencies; older source-bound results do not prove this successor. Full current-composition acceptance remains required.

## October 26, 2026: held resource-status freshness review fix

The reviewer found that memory and disk cards only used backend stale flags and connection state, so a still-connected browser holding an old status could continue to show “to lease” after those budgets expired. The web view now applies the same local elapsed-age calculation to CPU, memory and disk, and the Resources freshness check redraws when any one of their known/unknown states changes. A regression holds a connected status across staggered memory, disk and CPU deadlines; each “to lease” field becomes unavailable at its own deadline while promises remain visible and the original status object is unchanged. The complete web suite passes 201 tests; JavaScript syntax and staged diff checks pass. This is a browser presentation freshness fix, not a new backend admission rule or terminal layout change. The native observe full-suite counter limitation recorded below remains unchanged.

## October 26, 2026: terminal remaining-capacity parity

The complete `internal/tui` Go suite passes with one CPU and native loopback access. Focused tests verify remaining RAM/swap/state-filesystem headlines, available-RAM history rather than used-RAM history, known zero versus unknown, stale and disconnected monitor readings, authoritative CPU/memory/disk admission values, held-budget aging, promises and shortfalls. `go vet ./internal/tui` and a one-CPU CLI build pass. The first full-suite attempt in the restricted sandbox failed only at two private-backend tests because localhost bind returned `operation not permitted`; the same complete suite then passed with loopback permission. Deterministic 140×42 and 70×28 previews, plus the disconnected preview, were inspected for label order, fit and stale presentation.

The built CLI passes the real PTY dashboard workflow with a disposable backend: live remaining labels, Resources “To lease,” six-view navigation, mouse selection, resize/compact feed, guarded lease/message controls, I/O selectors, disconnected remaining values and stale budget notices, and terminal restoration on normal and signal exit. A counted-pool lease fixture keeps this presentation test independent of current host CPU pressure; the first run's CPU=1 lease failed admission under real host pressure and a later CPU=0 attempt was invalid without another claim. The separate real PTY color-profile workflow passes TrueColor, 256-color, 16-color and NO_COLOR plus compact labels, resize and decline. These are presentation and workflow checks, not proof of physical capacity or complete product scope.

## October 25, 2026: routed SSH three-node integration

The new `internal/meshsshrelay` carrier/policy tests pass with actual loopback WebSocket hops, nested pinned TLS, opaque transit bytes, route replay refusal and active edge revocation. A compiled server fixture runs three mesh services with B↔A and A↔C only. A separately reviewed transit grant and C's original-B destination grant admit a real SSH command from B through A to C; output matches, the destination's exact key receipt names B and the virtual destination grant pair, and C has no direct B enrollment. The same routed control path returns an exact original-source nonexecution seal. Removing A's relay grant closes an active held stream, settles its local transit hold only after copy/watch/cancellation joins, and rejects a subsequent identity stream. Its live charge records B, C, the route class and workspace. A source-side implementation preserves the original routed pin for lookup/seal and probes candidate paths before execution, but the public CLI workflow has not been exercised end to end by this fixture. Actual independent macOS/Linux CLI workflows, changed-route recovery, sync worker and encrypted throughput remain required before claiming full specification acceptance.

The isolated routed SSH hardening worktree at base `7f6812f` adds a per-relay reviewed CPU/memory promise and shared bidirectional byte rate. A legacy unbudgeted grant stays readable for replacement/revocation but refuses forwarding. Focused normal tests pass across `internal/meshsshrelay`, `internal/server` and `internal/cli`, including exact CLI recheck cancellation. The callback and watcher race test passes, and the held-callback test passes three normal repetitions. A first final focused run failed because the three-node fixture's destination CPU observation aged out before its one-core command; starting the destination's normal sampler and using a 0.1-core test claim made the three-node test pass twice. The later failed-admission regression additionally proves a timed-out reservation remains charged while its abort callback is held, then settles after join. Affected package vet passes. These tests do not exercise the public `arm ssh` command, native Linux SSH relay, alternate-route recovery or encrypted throughput.

The final focused routed/direct SSH tests pass across `internal/meshsshrelay`, `internal/sshservice`, `internal/server` and `internal/cli`; affected package vet and compilation pass. The earlier broad local suite first failed because the sandbox denied loopback listeners and Unix sockets. With native socket permission, `meshsshrelay`, `workspace` and `sshservice` passed, while the full server package retained three unrelated current-worktree failures: budget mesh lease projection rejected a node-stop `ttl_ns` field, and two operator/server fixtures reported insufficient live CPU capacity. CLI compilation in that run overlapped the directory-pin edit and failed transiently; a clean affected-package compile and focused suite pass afterward. This is not a full green repository gate.

## October 25, 2026: audited embedded rsync staging engine

Integrated a source-preserving licensed `gokrazy/rsync` v0.3.8 fork through a local module replacement and a four-role stream API. Registry dependencies are checksum-verified; no host rsync executable or system sshd is required by the engine. Independent review reproduces/fixes a negative long-name panic, a fresh-file hash worker surviving early wire errors, final-data/EOF and zero-progress mistakes, and missing changed-size enforcement on the delta path. Focused fork normal/race tests cover 42 named runs across 17 roots, including corrupt/range/literal data, exact source ownership/closure and installed-rsync client/server push/pull/delta. Wrapper races and affected fork/wrapper vet pass.

The current full CGo-free fork suite passes outside five sandbox-denied integration packages; those five localhost-listener scopes are rerun with native permission and all pass. The original denied run remains failed. Actual macOS interoperability uses the installed openrsync implementation. Current Linux ARM64 sender, receiver and stream test executables are built CGo-free and run under an owned half-core scope. The machine has no installed rsync, so a first preflight fails without running those tests; its exact temporary directory is independently confirmed absent. A distribution rsync 3.4.1/protocol32 plus libpopt is then downloaded/verified and unpacked into a private temporary root, without system package installation. All three native test programs pass, including both real-rsync roles and push/pull deltas; the native wrapper records exit zero and inactive scope. The outer SSH process nevertheless returns one. A separate read-only postflight returns zero and confirms both exact directory absence and inactive scope; the original outer SSH failure is preserved, not relabeled successful transport acceptance.

A separate real ARM backend fixture carries the engine through meshstream TLS, embedded SSH and `Manager.AcquireSSH` into an owned destination test worker. Fresh push, changed-file delta and pull verify SHA-256 contents and exact terminal destination lease/key/fence receipts. Normal execution passes (3.02s), then an independent race run passes (7.60s body/9.072s package). Reported rsync-protocol bytes fall from 884,980 to 23,688 for the edited file; these are not authoritative encrypted-network counters. The fixture reports its unrelated native Stop capability unavailable; this test claims SSH/lease receipts, not Stop or public source-intent orchestration. No production sync worker/CLI, Linux-through-ARM or rsync relay acceptance is implied by that fixture.

A local 16 MiB unencrypted pipe sample reports approximately 177 MB/s, and small edited files send substantially fewer bytes than full copies. Neither establishes encrypted SSH throughput or the 85–90% link target. Acceptance is limited to private staging transfers and actual peer interoperability. Protocol MD4 and peer-reported statistics do not prove the expected modern-hashed tree or resource settlement. Native publication/CAS/quarantine, leased CLI, both-endpoint receipts/baselines, bidirectional conflicts/removals, complete routed SSH CLI and sync acceptance, and final performance acceptance remain required.

## October 25, 2026: contained managed CPU credit

CPU sampling now brackets each host read with CPU-only managed process cuts. Only the previous post-read to current pre-read increment is considered. The reviewed initial positive scope is native ARM64 Darwin 25/XNU 12377 release recount accounting without translation and with a complete host CPU inventory. Raw Mach counters, kernel unique process/parent identities and conservative per-job rounding avoid borrowing an unrelated usage window or another lease’s idle promise. Native-family applicability is a same-family source inference from Apple’s tagged release, not running-kernel attestation. Linux, other unverified Darwin families, restricted affinity and finite quotas retain zero credit; this stage does not complete broader credit or automatic pressure pausing.

Focused Manager races pass actual owned-process fixtures with controlled candidate inputs: renewal/self-counting, spare admission, per-job caps, duplicate/native binding refusal, replacement, original Wait return, stale/unknown/invalid numbers and actual worker settlement. The first release assertion failed because it treated requested release as immediate process join; the corrected test waits on the original job’s own completion while promises remain charged through ending. Normal CPU regressions pass (29.667s), peer privacy/TUI checks pass, and an obsolete CLI explanation assertion is updated and passes. Affected vet and offline guide/installer round-trip checks pass. A temporary compile attempt during the coordinated removal failed on a not-yet-written guard; it is retained, not a passing test.

Native macOS race acceptance separately verifies a real root/child/grandchild ancestry tree and a four-second one-core descendant. The complete sampler earns approximately 0.666667 cores with all three identities matched, quantum 240000, a 36-tick margin for 18 CPUs and maximum host-total delta 120. Original helpers all Wait successfully. A normal running Manager, with no injected observations/candidates, admits a separate bounded eight-second one-core helper and reports idle 3.969 + credit 0.785 = pool 4.754, promise 1 and availability 3.754. Renewal succeeds; original helper exits zero, its job joins, credit/promises return to zero and Manager Close succeeds. Initial sandbox identity denial and a native Sysctl setup compilation error are retained; corrected current gates pass.

Actual Linux in the reused one-vCPU VM verifies fresh headroom with explicitly zero managed credit, original sleep-helper Wait zero, and current parser/initial-namespace controls. A half-core native Manager scope observes approximately 0.497 → 0.002 → 0.459 core-equivalents: external pressure refuses direct admission, queues with the CPU blocker, recovers FIFO, counts promises once for renewal and releases to zero. Native command, helpers, Manager and SSH close successfully; scopes are inactive and remote test images removed. The VM is gracefully shut down and its original QEMU process returns zero. CGo-free Linux ARM64 build and Linux ARM64/Darwin AMD64 collector compilation pass; compilation is not execution on AMD64.

The successor finite model checks 12,281 distinct states and TLAPM discharges 60 obligations. Five actual Go/SQL histories provide 196 raw cuts, 69 complete states, 64 checked transitions and five final witnesses; 24 altered histories are refused. Earlier finite/proof/producer failures remain retained. The last original outer run exits one after all Go cases and all five positive TLC paths pass, because its new negative control changed only one of two duplicate samples and left the other valid. Correcting the passive control to change both copies preserves byte-equal positive projections and complete TLC rows/edges/finals; the passive recheck returns zero without replaying Go or the prover. These composed results cover accounting/ownership decisions with controlled native inputs, not collector accuracy or kernel scheduling guarantees.

All 197 web tests pass. A fresh native backend and private in-app-browser tab render the credited-use row, real 0.25-core cooperative promise with owner/reason, and plain accounting popup. The bare lease correctly earns zero managed credit. The lease is released; explicit fixture Stop and original backend Wait return zero, its endpoint disappears and the test tab closes. Screenshots stay in ignored `.cache/cpu-credit-render/`. Current Charm presentation is covered by focused formatting/layout checks; native Terminal.app rendering of the new row is not claimed.

Source inspection distinguishes two existing paths. In `internal/manager/manager.go`, activation records a UTC expiry, renewal compares wall time with that persisted expiry, and tick expires active/provisioning leases by the same wall-time comparison. A backwards wall change can defer that comparison. `internal/manager/scheduling.go` separately retains elapsed deadlines and detects wall rollback for queued latest-start waits; it does not establish equivalent protection for ordinary active lifetimes.

The specification requires sleep to count and clock changes not to silently extend leases. The workspace-clock candidate and its scoped source/model gates do not prove this separate manager requirement. Record active expiry/renewal, suspend, rollback, restart and provider live-hold eligibility as explicit follow-up work. This checkpoint is source inspection only: no system clock change, injected-clock test, new native workflow or active-lifetime acceptance is claimed.

These use actual private stores, durable writers and local test transports; they do not establish cross-host SSH acceptance.

Independent regressions reproduced three findings. A selected blocker removed by refresh caused a terminal slice panic (0.554s). Inspecting while another read was pending discarded the editor without opening the requested operation (0.598s). The reconnect resolver and independent historical reader accepted a malformed explicit local IP (0.478s/0.601s); their loops stopped at that first failing case. The reviewed successors preserve selection by exact key, require explicit reselection after removal, retain the editor until a read is accepted, and independently validate bounded IPv4/IPv6 literals before setup or archival acceptance. The original panic probe expected automatic selection of a different remaining key; that expectation was explicitly replaced with the stronger deliberate-reselection policy, while its failing source remains retained. The pending-read assertions remain unchanged.

Both disjoint five-file corrections pass exact preimage checks over the original checkpoint. Independent corrected terminal/review/viewport races and stronger exact-key controls passed 1.742 s. The composed full coordinator race suite passed 19.933 s, selected independent database readers passed 8.287 s and CLI checks passed 5.361 s, including both original address regressions, valid-address controls and offline exact-key behavior. Affected six-package vet also passed. Source/evidence receipts remain private. Actual native/Linux SSH reconnect, commit-then-lost-reply recovery, rendered browser/PTY acceptance, the enrollment-generation model/actual-trace/complete-proof gates, broader reset and final current-tree composition remain pending. No remote administrative grant, user backend or authenticated agent session was changed.

## New interface and mount acceptance requirements

The operator requires polished web interfaces, Charm/Lip Gloss terminal windows and tables with truecolor/highcolor styling where supported, and matching functionality. Reduced-color/no-color, keyboard/mouse, compact layouts, contrast and non-color state cues remain part of acceptance. The current terminal polish and mesh interface work is in progress; this requirement is not a claim that complete parity or its visual gates have passed.

Mounted-share performance is an early selection gate: initially target approximately 85–90% of same-host encrypted streaming throughput for large sequential transfers, measuring cold reads and durable writes separately, with cache conditions and storage/CPU limits disclosed. Metadata-heavy and real build workloads need separate measurements. No throughput benchmark or performance acceptance has passed yet; tiny synthetic feasibility reads do not establish it.

## Embedded mesh access proposal — research only

The subsequently approved [SSH/mounted-share scheme](MESH-ACCESS-PROPOSAL.md) was checked against the current mesh contract, native macOS mount manpages, primary OpenSSH/Linux FUSE/NFS documentation, Apple's SMB client source and rclone's local NFS mount implementation. Independent review identified extra SSH channel admission, cross-share filehandle/path confinement, other-local-user access and partial cross-authority setup as required gates; the scheme includes them. Operator approval authorizes staged implementation, not a validation pass. No embedded SSH/file server, mount, credential mechanism or protocol dependency was implemented or acceptance-tested by the design checkpoint. Actual non-root Linux/macOS mount feasibility, safe local authentication and writable semantics remain unverified; source precedent is not a runtime pass. No system configuration, privileged helper or service changes were made for the proposal.

Agentic lease reviews are specified and planned, but not implemented or validated. Acceptance must cover exact-session and polling delivery, bounded/coalesced scheduling, restart deduplication, stale or duplicate replies, and unchanged expiry/enforcement deadlines unless an authorized lifecycle operation succeeds.

## October 21, 2026: shared native WebSocket session

Eleven real-loopback WebSocket/client tests passed under the race detector, including three repeated runs and vet. Tests cover out-of-order concurrent replies, max-int64 values, 100 unconsumed telemetry pages with explicit coalescing gaps, durable events and request bounds, overflow/resynchronization, dropped mutation responses without retries, and cancellation/late response isolation. Review fixed monitor identity changing before its request succeeded and context-insensitive waiting for frame writes. Rejected replacements preserve telemetry; concurrent monitor controls serialize while ordinary requests proceed; successful acknowledgements switch identities in wire order, unsubscribe clears queued telemetry, unsent cancellations preserve the stream, and sent ambiguous cancellations close for resynchronization. Deterministic held-write-gate checks require cancelled calls to return before the gate is released. This is a transport foundation, not TUI/reconnect completion; consumers must reload snapshots after failure and never replay mutations automatically.

Claude used its existing keychain sign-in and a temporary project; native session history may retain the dedicated test transcripts. JSON results are written to `.cache/live-agent-results.json` for local inspection.

## Scope of this result

The October 20 checks establish the first local backend and CLI milestone. They do not establish APFS allocation accuracy, physical usage enforcement, managed artifact reclamation, GPU or port-provider behavior, native daemon services, web/TUI functionality, or remote scheduling. The October 21 additions above establish limited read-only observations, not exact reclaimable extents or enforcement. Remaining work is tracked in [PLAN.md](PLAN.md), [IMPLEMENTATION.md](IMPLEMENTATION.md), and [SPEC.md](../SPEC.md).

## October 25: native routed SSH public CLI and closed pin read

A private QEMU Debian ARM64 guest with one vCPU and a complete native Linux CPU
hierarchy ran CGo-free binaries built from this worktree. The original guest
disk and firmware were not modified; the run used an ignored private qcow2
overlay and firmware copy. The focused three-node server test and all
`internal/meshsshrelay` tests passed in the guest. The new routed pin
round-trip/partial-extension regression passed on macOS and native Linux;
affected `sshservice`/CLI vet passed. The public CLI fixture is
`scripts/test-routed-ssh.py`.

The final public fixture starts real daemons B, A and C, admits all three into
one reviewed workspace, keeps B and C without a direct enrollment, grants A
transit and C execution for original B, then executes B→A→C with exact stdout,
stderr and exit 23. Original-key lookup finds its local receipt and a same-key
retry refuses execution. An opaque ciphertext relay drops a response after a
separate command writes its marker; the source returns a transport error,
retains a local completion receipt, and lookup/retry leave the marker exactly
once. A second drop after source intent but before admission returns
`ssh_outcome_unknown`; a different key is refused until an exact-key
nonexecution seal succeeds, with no destination marker. Revoking C's grant
refuses a fresh command. After renewed grant and graph report convergence, a
fourth member D receives an independent transit grant; A's grant is revoked,
public dry-run selects B→D→C, and the actual command returns the expected
output. B and C still have no direct enrollment. The final guest harness exits
zero and removes its successful private fixture.

An earlier four-node diagnostic exposed that a stored routed pin could not be
read on a later command: the strict direct-pin decoder rejected its route
fields. The fix accepts only the complete six-field route extension and keeps
direct pins strict. A single earlier 0.1-CPU CLI attempt returned an uncertain
OpenSSH exec refusal with no destination SSH operation; the final accepted
fixture uses a 0.05-CPU command promise on the one-vCPU guest. Another early
alternate-path attempt used a stale graph snapshot; ordered D/C/B/A/B refresh
made the current alternate path visible. Broad macOS `sshservice` and CLI runs
inside this restricted sandbox failed on denied local socket/process
operations; they are not counted as product test failures or passing gates.
Native macOS public CLI, wider graph/partition, throughput, formal and sync
acceptance remain open. The guest received ACPI powerdown and the owned QEMU
process exited zero. No private or original disk holder remained; only the
private overlay, copied firmware, keys, socket and uploaded binaries were
removed. Test logs and their SHA-256 manifest remain in ignored
`.cache/routed-ssh-linux`; failed guest fixtures were discarded with the
private overlay. The original VM fixture was not modified.

## October 25: scoped native Linux routed SSH stdout throughput

The recorded optional `--benchmark` run of `scripts/test-routed-ssh.py` used a
private one-vCPU Debian ARM64 guest and real public CLI. It verified three
32-MiB `/dev/zero` stdout samples byte-for-byte and by SHA-256 over B→A→C
while B and C have no direct enrollment. Only afterward does it grant a
separate direct B↔C enrollment and verify three equal-size direct CLI samples.
The routed grant promises 0.05 CPU, 8 MiB transit memory and a shared
64-MiB/s byte rate; each SSH execution requests 0.05 CPU. Wall-clock timing
includes CLI startup, route selection, handshakes, execution and drain. All
six transfers and the successful fixture cleanup passed.

Routed samples were **16.104, 13.232 and 12.028 MiB/s** (median 13.232);
direct samples were **31.863, 33.098 and 37.557 MiB/s** (median 33.098).
The median routed/direct ratio is 39.98%. This is a warm same-guest loopback
comparison under QEMU's user network and one shared vCPU, not a physical
cross-host, macOS, cold-storage or sync measurement. It provides actual
throughput evidence and exposes a material performance gap; no 85–90% target
or broad performance acceptance is claimed. Guest logs record AArch64 Linux,
one eligible CPU and visible cgroup ancestry (`user.slice` and
`user-1000.slice` each reported `max 100000`). The original positive CPU
admission guard was unchanged.

The benchmark guest received ACPI powerdown; the owned QEMU process exited
zero, and no original or private disk holder remained. Its private overlay,
firmware copy, copied SSH material, QMP socket and uploaded binary were
removed. Ignored `.cache/routed-ssh-linux/benchmark-vm` retains the exact
measurement output, guest CPU-scope observations and SHA-256 log manifest.

Independent review then found two acceptance-artifact issues. A successful
fixture could return zero when `Node.stop()` failed, and benchmark output
hardcoded the original guest label for every environment. The harness now
requires every stop and fixture removal before exit zero or a PASS/benchmark
report, retains failed fixtures, preserves an original test exception during cleanup, and reports
observed OS/architecture/CPU scope plus the exact harness and CLI binary
SHA-256 for future measurements. Five local fake-node/relay tests cover stop
failure, original-error preservation, removal failure, environment probing
and binary hashing; they do not rerun the VM. The original benchmark samples
above are unchanged.

The earlier strict pin decoder also accepted all six route keys explicitly
present as empty values, silently treating them as a direct pin. It now
rejects that noncanonical extension while continuing to accept legacy direct
pins with the route keys absent. Focused direct/routed pin regressions pass;
no prior native Linux run is relabeled as testing this later correction.
The ignored validation manifest now hashes the exact final-source PASS log
and both historical harness source versions. The exact uploaded CLI binary
was deleted before hashing, so its SHA-256 remains explicitly unavailable in
both manifests; source hashes are not presented as binary proof.

## October 26: charged sync preparation and atomic admission handoff

Independent review reproduced two private-adapter defects: full native hashing
preceded resource admission, and a valid outgoing 4 MiB source could reserve
only 2 MiB of local disk. The successor grants a private preparation hold before
Manager probing/scanning/expensive validation, includes outgoing materialization
and control journal overhead, and binds held native device/fsid to the sampled
state-filesystem fsid and optional APFS pool. The original real-file counterexample
is retained as a passing regression. CPU, memory, disk and draining refusal tests
use a real special file that would fail if traversal were reached.

The original native phase joins before an exact-owner accounting handoff in the
same final sync/SSH/lease transaction. One-core host and one-core peer fixtures
admit the requested quarter-core worker without double-demanding preparation. A
real SQLite abort during handoff rolls back all final evidence while retaining
the original active preparation. Other tests cover generic marker refusal,
End/cancel before join, Close waiting for join, peer aggregate/expiry limits,
unknown scratch retention including automatic-settlement races, restart without
a PID-zero join inference, native/sample volume mismatch, read-only same-key
retry despite an unscannable changed root, and independent settled/reset refusal
for uncertain preparation. The Manager and private worker now share the exact
`arm sync-worker --protocol=1` command; the unversioned command is refused before
preparation. The schema-only reset fixture keeps its counted pool lease and
omits irrelevant CPU work; generic CPU fixtures and native admission remain.

The final focused native macOS race selection passes: Manager 54.968s, database
5.417s and all seven private worker roots 3.195s. Affected Manager/database/worker
vet also passes. Ignored review logs are retained at
`.cache/review-sync-admission/final-race.log` and
`.cache/review-sync-admission/final-vet.log`. An earlier broader selection
failed a redundant constant assertion's vet check (corrected) and
`TestSSHResetArchivesCompleteNamespaceAndStartsFreshAuthority` reopening a fresh
reset seed because the retired-mount check queried an absent leases table. Its
original log remains `.cache/review-sync-admission/broader-reset-failure.log`; regression
attribution and the complete composed reset suite remain separate work. No full
Manager/database-suite, Linux, formal, public dispatch, actual allocation/RSS
enforcement, end-to-end transfer or throughput pass is inferred.

The primary composition additionally passes selected Manager/database/worker
race tests (131.990s/10.022s/3.402s) and affected vet. Actual Linux ARM64 runs
the selected admission, preparation, pressure/relay and reset evidence cases
plus the complete worker package with zero failures/skips. All three uploaded

Native Manager observations are controlled for these transactional tests; this
